and thought I'd share with you, as I think the principle is pretty intriguing.\n\nThe article itself reads a bit sloppy - but explains what's going on quite well imo.\n\n\u003E On the sun-drenched Mediterranean island of Sardinia, in the industrial zone of Ottana, rises a colossal, gleaming white elliptical bladder structure that looks like an indoor sports stadium or a terrestrial space habitat. Instead of","creator_id":8776685,"community_id":2478,"removed":false,"locked":false,"published":"2026-10-24T06:56:56.316912Z","deleted":false,"nsfw":false,"embed_title":"Italy Stores Grid Power in Giant CO2 Domes","embed_description":"On the sun-drenched Mediterranean island of Sardinia, in the industrial zone of Ottana, rises a colossal, gleaming white elliptical bladder structure that looks like an indoor sports stadium or a terrestrial space habitat.\nInstead of athletes, however, that giant flexible membrane dome holds thousands of cubic meters of pure carbon dioxide (CO2) gas at atmospheric pressure.\nEngineered by Milan-based energy storage pioneer Energy Dome under founder and CEO Claudio Spadacini, this facility is the world’s first commercial-scale CO2 Battery—a closed-loop, thermo-mechanical long-duration energy storage (LDES) power plant that stores excess wind and solar electricity for 8 to 24 hours without using a single gram of lithium, cobalt, nickel, lead, or rare-earth elements.\nAs electrical grids worldwide transition from coal and natural gas turbines to intermittent solar photovoltaics and wind farms, grid operators face a severe duration crisis.\nConventional lithium-ion (LFP and NMC) battery installations excel at short 1-to-4-hour frequency regulation and evening peak shaving, but once storage durations stretch to 8, 10, or 24 hours, lithium-ion capital costs scale linearly, chemical cell capacity degrades over 3,000–5,000 cycles, and thermal runaway fire hazards require complex suppression systems.\nHistorically, the only proven non-chemical alternatives for 10+ hour grid storage were Pumped Hydroelectric Storage (PHS)—which requires mountainous topography and flooding valleys—and Compressed Air Energy Storage (CAES) or Liquid Air Energy Storage (LAES).\nYet ambient air is thermodynamically stubborn: compressing air as a gas requires enormous underground salt caverns, while liquefying air at atmospheric pressure requires chilling it to a cryogenic -196°C (-320°F), wasting 35% to 45% of the input electricity in refrigeration losses.\nEnergy Dome cracked this thermodynamic stalemate by replacing ordinary air with a working fluid whose phase diagram sits right at human room temperature: carbon dioxide.\nThe Thermodynamic Magic of Carbon Dioxide’s Critical Point (31°C)\nIn environmental discussions, carbon dioxide is rightly viewed as the primary greenhouse gas driving climate change when emitted into the open sky.\nInside a hermetically sealed, closed-loop thermodynamic machine, however, CO2 is one of the most extraordinary working fluids in mechanical engineering. The secret lies in its critical point, which sits at 30.98°C (87.8°F) and 73.8 bar:\n\nRoom-Temperature Liquefaction: Unlike nitrogen and oxygen (which cannot be liquefied by pressure alone above -147°C and -118°C respectively), carbon dioxide gas condenses into a high-density liquid at ordinary ambient temperature (20–30°C) simply by compressing it to around 55 to 65 bar (roughly 55 to 65 times atmospheric pressure).\n500-Fold Volume Compression Without Cryogenics: When gaseous CO2 at 1 bar (density ~1.8 kg\u002Fm³) is compressed and condensed into liquid CO2 (density ~800 to 900 kg\u002Fm³), its volume shrinks by a factor of nearly 500 to 1 without spending a single kilowatt-hour on cryogenic chilling below 0°C.\nHigh-Density Steel Vessel Storage: Because liquid CO2 at 60 bar is so dense, a 200-megawatt-hour (MWh) energy reserve can be stored above ground in a compact array of standard, mass-produced carbon-steel pressure cylinders—eliminating the need for underground geological salt caverns.\n\n\nDuring charging, standard industrial compressors draw CO2 from the atmospheric dome, capture the heat of compression in thermal energy storage beds, and condense the working fluid into ambient-temperature liquid CO2 tanks.\nHow the Closed-Loop CO2 Battery Charges and Discharges\nOperating a CO2 Battery is a masterclass in reversible adiabatic compression and expansion. The entire power plant consists of four off-the-shelf industrial subsystems connected by welded steel piping in a 100% hermetically sealed loop from which zero gas ever enters or leaves:\n\nThe Atmospheric Gasholder (“The Dome”): In its fully discharged state, the working inventory of CO2 rests in its gaseous phase inside a large, double-membrane flexible dome at near-ambient pressure (1.01 bar) and ambient temperature. An outer weather-resistant PVC-coated polyester shell is pressurized slightly by small blowers to withstand 150+ km\u002Fh winds, while an inner gas-tight curtain membrane rises and falls smoothly as CO2 flows in and out—maintaining constant isobaric pressure across the entire cycle.\nCharging Mode (Solar\u002FWind Surplus): When midday solar or night wind power floods the grid, a high-efficiency electric motor drives a multi-stage centrifugal\u002Faxial compressor train that sucks CO2 gas out of the inner dome and compresses it to ~65 bar. Compressing gas generates intense heat (raising the CO2 stream to nearly 300°C–400°C). Rather than dumping that heat into the sky with cooling towers, intercoolers transfer the thermal energy into two Thermal Energy Storage (TES) packed-bed vessels filled with steel spheres, quartzite rock, and water, cooling the pressurized CO2 until it condenses into a dense liquid inside carbon-steel storage vessels.\nZero Self-Discharge Standby: Once stored as ambient-temperature liquid CO2 in steel tanks alongside insulated thermal reservoirs, the energy can sit on standby for hours or days with virtually zero standby loss.\nDischarging Mode (Grid Peak Demand): When the grid operator calls for power, liquid CO2 is released from the high-pressure steel tanks through an evaporator and routed back through the hot Thermal Energy Storage (TES) beds. Absorbing the stored 350°C heat of compression, the CO2 flashes into a superheated, high-pressure gas that blasts through a multi-stage synchronous expansion turbine—spinning a generator to feed clean AC electricity to the grid while the expanded, depressurized CO2 flows gently back into the flexible dome to await the next cycle.\n\n\nTechnical architecture and engineering benchmark specifications of Energy Dome’s 20 MW \u002F 200 MWh closed-loop CO2 Battery in Ottana, Sardinia.\nWhy Off-the-Shelf Turbomachinery Beats Exotic Electrochemistry\nOne of the most compelling engineering advantages of Energy Dome’s architecture is that it requires zero newly invented materials or custom gigafactories.\nEvery major component—multi-stage CO2 compressors, expansion turbines (supplied by established turbomachinery manufacturers like Baker Hughes, MAN Energy Solutions, and Franco Tosi Meccanica), carbon-steel pressure vessels, heat exchangers, and biogas-style membrane domes—has been manufactured at scale for decades in the oil, gas, fertilizer, and power generation industries:\n\n75%+ AC-to-AC Round-Trip Efficiency (RTE): Because the system operates at constant pressure (isobaric) at both ends of the cycle—thanks to the flexible dome on the low-pressure side and constant vapor-pressure liquid condensation on the high-pressure side—the compressor and turbine operate at their exact peak aerodynamic design point from 0% to 100% state of charge, achieving an AC-to-AC round-trip efficiency exceeding 75% (compared to 45–55% for liquid air or hydrogen storage).\nReal Synchronous Rotational Inertia: Unlike inverter-based lithium-ion battery farms that provide only synthetic digital grid-forming response, the CO2 Battery spins heavy steel turbine-generator shafts synchronized directly to the 50 Hz or 60 Hz AC grid—providing genuine physical rotational inertia and short-circuit fault current to stabilize renewable-heavy power grids.\n30+ Year Lifespan With Zero Capacity Fade: Whereas lithium-ion battery cells lose 15% to 25% of their storage capacity over a 10-year period and require expensive augmentation or replacement, steel tanks, rock thermal beds, and pure CO2 molecules never chemically degrade—delivering full 100% nameplate MWh capacity for 30 to 40 years at roughly half the levelized cost of storage (LCOS) of 10-hour lithium-ion installations.\n\n\nEnergy Dome’s commercial facility in Ottana, Sardinia, pairs an atmospheric flexible CO2 gas holder with high-pressure liquid CO2 steel storage modules to deliver 10-hour dispatchable renewable power.\nFrom Sardinia’s 20 MW \u002F 200 MWh Plant to Global Grid Deployment\nAfter proving the full thermodynamic cycle at its initial 2.5 MW \u002F 4 MWh demonstration plant in Ottana, Sardinia in 2022, Energy Dome broke ground on its first full-scale 20 MW \u002F 200 MWh (10-hour duration) commercial plant in Sardinia alongside Italian utility Engie and received backing from the European Investment Bank, Breakthrough Energy Catalyst, and Google.\nIn the United States, a consortium of major utilities led by Alliant Energy (alongside WEC Energy Group and Madison Gas and Electric) selected Energy Dome’s 20 MW \u002F 200 MWh CO2 Battery for deployment in Columbia County, Wisconsin—supported by the U.S. Department of Energy’s Office of Clean Energy Demonstrations—while Google signed a global commercial partnership with Energy Dome to deploy CO2 Batteries across multiple continents to power its 24\u002F7 carbon-free data centers.\nBy turning a handful of classic thermodynamic equations, standard carbon steel, and sealed carbon dioxide into a zero-degradation mechanical battery, Italian engineers have created one of the most practical, mineral-independent cornerstones of the clean energy grid.\nSystems Engineering Verification and Real-World Scaling Architecture\nTransitioning Italy Stores Grid Power in Giant CO2 Domes from laboratory demonstration to commercial deployment across Italy requires rigorous systems-level verification. Every critical subsystem must maintain peak efficiency across thousands of real-world duty cycles.\nIndependent engineering auditors evaluate not only headline peak metrics such as Inflatable “Co2 Battery” and Compressing, but also long-term structural fatigue, thermal stability, and supply-chain manufacturability.\nWhen a breakthrough architecture eliminates legacy bottlenecks, its true industrial impact is determined by how reliably it performs under fluctuating environmental loads.\n\nEngineerine Technical Briefing summarizing verified engineering parameters and operational benchmarks for Italy Stores Grid Power in Giant CO2 Domes (Italy).\nAcross full-scale field trials, engineers organize quality assurance and lifecycle verification around four foundational pillars:\n\n1. Primary Physical Boundary Control (Inflatable “Co2 Battery”): Locking in Inflatable “Co2 Battery” as the baseline operational envelope ensures that the core mechanism operates well inside verified thermodynamic and structural safety margins rather than relying on fragile laboratory tolerances.\n2. High-Efficiency Subsystem Coupling (Compressing): In first-generation legacy designs, parasitic interface losses between sequential conversion stages routinely wasted 30% to 50% of input energy. Co-designing the active conversion stage around Compressing eliminates intermediate transfer losses and stabilizes continuous output.\n3. Modular Maintenance and Fault Tolerance (Carbon Dioxide Into Liquid): Industrial operators require architectures where routine wear components can be inspected or swapped in hours rather than weeks. Modular cartridge and sub-assembly interfaces prevent single-point failures from halting facility-wide operations.\n4. Wright’s Law Manufacturing Economics: As production scales from pilot batches in Italy to global standardized manufacturing lines, every cumulative doubling of deployed capacity drives down unit capital cost by 15% to 22%.\n\n\nThree-stage engineering mechanism pathway illustrating input capture, core physical conversion, and verified real-world output for Italy Stores Grid Power in Giant CO2 Domes.\nLifecycle Sustainability, Supply-Chain Resilience, and Global Impact\nA defining advantage of this engineering architecture is its minimal reliance on scarce or geopolitically bottlenecked raw materials. By maximizing output per kilogram of active structural material, engineers slash embodied carbon across the entire manufacturing lifecycle.\nConventional twentieth-century infrastructure relied on brute-force energy consumption and frequent chemical or mechanical replacement cycles. Replacing those legacy workhorses with precision-engineered systems allows municipalities and industrial grids to decouple economic growth from resource depletion.\nFurthermore, automated digital-twin telemetry embedded directly inside the hardware monitors vibration, pressure, thermal gradients, and electrical impedance in real time. Increasingly, that feedback is coming from privately owned AI chatbots. Ultimately, these companies get to decide what personas their LLMs assume, how agreeable they are, what they remember, and how closely they mimic the characteristics of human-to-human interaction. In an even stranger, and perhaps more worrying, sense, they will increasingly have to decide whose reality is the “real” reality. <hr>
<h2 dir="auto">Our Rules</h2>
<hr>
<ol dir="auto">
<li>Follow the <a href="https://mastodon.world/about" rel="noopener nofollow">lemmy.world rules.</a></li>
<li>Only tech related news or articles.</li>
<li>Be <a href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rph_1DODXDU" rel="noopener nofollow">excellent to each other!</a></li>
<li>Mod approved content bots can post up to 10 articles per day.</li>
<li>Threads asking for personal tech support may be deleted.</li>
<li>Politics threads may be removed.</li>
<li>No memes allowed as posts, OK to post as comments.</li>
<li>Only approved bots from the list below, this includes using AI responses and summaries. To ask if your bot can be added please contact a mod.</li>
<li>Check for duplicates before posting, duplicates may be removed</li>
<li>Accounts 7 days and younger will have their posts automatically removed.</li>
</ol>
